The launcher-level deadline integration described in section 9a may also require a small future `run.sh` change, while preserving its public interface.\r\n\r\n| Runtime module | Responsibility | Explicit boundary |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| `solution.py` â€” existing | Parse the existing two arguments; hold simple configuration; run stages in order; maintain coverage and run summary; enforce global deadline; assemble, validate, and atomically write output. | Does not interpret language or read answers. No separate orchestrator, config, or exporter module. |\r\n| `data.py` â€” new | Read the three allowed inputs; retain raw messages; index IDs; sort chats; create windows; retrieve original evidence and candidate context. | No API calls and no directory-wide file discovery. |\r\n| `reasoning.py` â€” new | Keep a small set of versioned prompt templates and model-response contracts; extract observations; resolve organisations/people/chats/goals; review task links and help choices in batches. | Returns proposed decisions with evidence. Does not silently mutate task state. No separate module per output category. |\r\n| `tasks.py` â€” new | Store task records and chronological events; generate candidate matches; request semantic review through `reasoning.py`; validate and apply supported operations; produce final task states. | Owns task identity/state. Similar titles or shared source IDs alone do not merge tasks. |\r\n| `llm.py` â€” existing | Be the single HTTP client; read required environment variables; retain response metadata; track calls/tokens/time; perform bounded retries; deduplicate identical successful requests in memory. | Only runtime network boundary. No second endpoint/model and no duplicate retry layer. |\r\n| `validation.py` â€” new | Check intermediate response structure, evidence references, final schema rules, and semantic invariants. | Never imports `check.py` or any evaluator. Does not repair meaning by inventing values. |\r\n\r\nKeep `run.sh`'s public interface, `schema.json`, and `check.py`. Do not change the schema or checker to accommodate predictions. Tests can use the standard-library test runner and synthetic fixtures. A separate evaluation framework is unnecessary for version 1: after approval, the supplied checker can evaluate frozen outputs as an explicit separate command.\r\n\r\nDependency direction: `solution.py` coordinates the other modules; `tasks.py` may call the task-review function in `reasoning.py`; `reasoning.py` uses `llm.py` and `validation.py`; `data.py` and `validation.py` do not import semantic stages. This avoids circular dependencies. Use ordinary dictionaries and a few small record definitions near their owning module rather than a separate type hierarchy.\r\n\r\nPrefer an already available, compatible offline schema dependency over writing a validator unnecessarily. `validation.py` should be a thin adapter around that dependency plus the project's evidence, coverage, date, and task-state checks. Read the actual `schema.json`; do not copy its enums into a second contract. This does not add a seventh application module.\r\n\r\nRead-only inspection found local Windows package metadata for `jsonschema` 4.26.0, requiring Python >=3.10 and transitive dependencies including `attrs`, `jsonschema-specifications`, `referencing`, and `rpds-py`. This confirms local metadata presence only: no import was executed, and neither working imports nor availability on the target Linux environment has been verified. The starter allows Python 3.9 or later, so this installed version must not silently raise the submission's minimum Python version or be assumed portable from Windows.\r\n\r\nMake one packaging decision before implementing validation: if a compatible, pinned validator and its dependencies are available offline on the chosen supported Linux interpreter, use it and verify it with network disabled. Do not copy platform-specific Windows dependencies into Linux or install from the internet during grading. Verify use of Draft 2020-12 and keep schema resolution local. No format extras are needed for this schema; project date/time checks remain explicit in Python because the supplied schema does not enforce those formats.\r\n\r\nOnly if that offline dependency cannot be supplied practically, choose a small custom validator for the exact used vocabulary: `type`, `enum`, `required`, `properties`, `additionalProperties`, `items`, `minItems`, `maxItems`, and `oneOf`. Treat title/description/schema-identification fields as metadata and fail clearly on unsupported validation keywords. Test every rule, nullable values, nested objects, and the exactly-one chat target. This is a scoped implementation, not full JSON Schema support. Do not build both validators for production or silently switch when an import fails: record the chosen backend/version, and fail configuration if that backend is unavailable. An already working local dependency can serve as a development reference for synthetic cases if the custom route is chosen, without becoming a runtime dependency.\r\n\r\n**2. Exact end-to-end data flow.**\r\n\r\nThe proposed normal run has the following order. Model stages are bounded batches, not one call per person or message.\r\n\r\n| Step | Input â†’ operation â†’ result | Model calls? |\r\n|---|---|---|\r\n| 0 | Arguments/environment â†’ validate configuration, start monotonic deadline, create empty budget ledger â†’ run context. | None; an approved compatibility check can be a separately counted setup job. |\r\n| 1 | `chats.jsonl`, `messages.jsonl`, `seed.json` â†’ validate, preserve raw text/IDs, index, sort â†’ input store and end-of-data time. | None. |\r\n| 2 | Per-chat messages â†’ bounded windows with overlap and explicit primary coverage â†’ extraction-job manifest and initial call/token estimate. | None. |\r\n| 3 | Each window plus relevant seed facts â†’ shared extraction â†’ relationship/organisation observations, goal candidates, task creations, and task-update events. | One request per window/job, plus bounded recovery. |\r\n| 4 | Valid observations â†’ merge overlap duplicates and build compact evidence packets â†’ global observation store. | None. |\r\n| 5a | Organisation observations plus seed names â†’ reconcile actual organisations and aliases â†’ canonical organisation records and IDs. | Batched context-resolution requests. |\r\n| 5b | Person/chat observations plus canonical organisations â†’ resolve circles, links, care relationships, and chat targets â†’ people and chat arrays. | Batched context-resolution requests; direct-chat defaults and seed circles use Python. |\r\n| 5c | Goal observations plus original evidence â†’ merge, reject unsupported intentions, choose allowed categories â†’ at most ten goals. | Batched context-resolution requests; skip only if extraction produced no candidates and coverage succeeded. |\r\n| 6 | Task creations/update events plus resolved people/chat context â†’ same-chat matching, cross-chat review, chronological replay â†’ task ledger with final states. | Batched task-review requests only where interpretation is needed. |\r\n| 7 | Final open tasks plus responsibility/context â†’ assist/remind/leave decisions â†’ help details attached to open tasks. | Batched final-decision requests. Closed tasks require none. |\r\n| 8 | All five results â†’ field allowlist, actual-schema validation, semantic checks, stable sorting â†’ one validated JSON object. | None. |\r\n| 9 | Validated JSON â†’ temporary file beside requested output â†’ final validation and atomic replacement â†’ output file and concise run diagnostics. | None. |\r\n\r\nSteps 5aâ€“5c use functions and variants of one context-resolution prompt family, not three separate subsystems. Task-review and help requests share another small prompt family but keep their result types distinct. The shared extraction pass is the only routine pass over all raw messages. Later requests contain compact observations and just the relevant original evidence.\r\n\r\nLarge decision sets are partitioned by entities or chats. Cross-partition organisation aliases receive a bounded merge review before IDs are finalised. People can legitimately link to several organisations; conflicting profile observations are reviewed rather than letting the last response overwrite earlier facts. A final consistency sweep checks duplicate organisations and conflicting chat targets. No unbounded recursive merging loop is allowed.\r\n\r\n**3. Minimal input/evidence contract.**\r\n\r\nThe loader explicitly opens only the three allowed input filenames. It does not enumerate the input directory, search for JSON files, check whether an answers directory exists, or import `check.py`. Seed facts are allowed input; reference labels are not. Resolve the allowed filenames to regular input files, and reject paths redirected into an answers directory. Inference modules receive parsed records, not an unrestricted dataset-directory handle they can search later.\r\n\r\nRetain original message IDs, chat IDs, sender IDs, timestamps, kind, text, and quoted ID. Sort by timestamp with original input position as a stable tie-breaker. A tie-breaker makes processing reproducible; it does not prove a real temporal order between simultaneous events. Set `as_of` to the latest input timestamp. Use a separately documented timezone policy for relative dates; Asia/Kolkata remains an assumption to confirm, not a fact inferred from every message.\r\n\r\nA window contains primary messages to extract and a small amount of overlapping context. Every message or span of a very long message has exactly one primary assignment; overlap does not count as additional coverage. Long text is split into spans retaining the same message ID, not cut off silently. Missing replies remain null. Media placeholders provide no attachment contents. No solution execution, model calls, checker runs, answer-file access, or development-dataset window run occurred in this correction.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Primary coverage: plan requirement versus implemented behaviour\\r\\n\\r\\nThe approved plan explicitly places primary assignment in Stage A. Section 11's Stage A row includes long-message spans and states: â€œEvery primary message/span is covered onceâ€. Section 3 requires exactly one primary assignment per message or long-message span, with overlap excluded from extra coverage. Section 1 assigns window creation to `data.py`; section 2 separates input loading (step 1), window/job creation (step 2), and extraction (step 3). These are plan facts, not a newly proposed redesign.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Coverage layer | Meaning | Owner and current status |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Loading/indexing | Which nonblank input records were read, parsed, retained, rejected, and uniquely indexed? | Stage A, `data.py`: implemented. A successful load does not prove extraction happened. |\\r\\n| Primary window/span assignment | Does each message, or every span of a long message, belong to exactly one primary extraction window? Context overlap must not count twice; spans must preserve original message IDs. | Stage A, `data.py`: implemented and tested; validation checks original-body intervals independently of splitting. |\\r\\n| Processing/extraction | Did each required window/job complete with validated coverage acknowledgement, rather than fail or remain pending? | Stage E extraction, with Stage G orchestration/completion checks in `solution.py`: future work. Acknowledgement alone does not prove that the model found every relevant fact. |\\r\\n\\r\\nUnsplit messages have one primary window; a long message can span multiple windows, with each nonoverlapping span assigned exactly once under the same original ID. Empty bodies get one [0, 0) span. Undated messages are retained; failed/incomplete input cannot pass `WindowPlan.require_complete()`, even when every indexed message has an assignment. Rejected source lines remain explicit and are not given fabricated evidence IDs.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe validator now enforces context against a bounded ordered per-chat tail, rather than global earlier-primary membership. Nonempty context must be a contiguous immediate suffix in order, with length at most the configured `overlap_spans`; empty context is allowed. This tail may cross earlier window boundaries, matching the unchanged builder. The builder passes its limit to validation, and the coverage report records that limit for later explicit revalidation. No cleanup was attempted again in this turn.\\r\\n- Original parsed dictionaries remain mutable; later consumers must avoid altering evidence records. Unknown dates remain undated rather than guessed.\\r\\n- Window limits are configurable Python-character/span bounds, not token budgets: defaults are 4,000 primary characters, 32 primary spans, and up to 2 preceding context spans. Fixed character splits can cross words or Unicode grapheme boundaries; original text is retained exactly and can be reconstructed.\\r\\n- The coverage dictionary is a snapshot. The standalone CLI still reports loading only.\\r\\n\\r\\nStop here pending approval.
